{"parentUuid":"0bd2110c-2e3a-43d6-88e0-aad33bf8e919","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"27385096-c98e-406e-9309-88c9790ffc82","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms/.claude/skills/code-review\n\nTwo-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:\n\n- **Standards**: does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?\n- **Spec**: does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / spec?\n\nBoth axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.\n\nThe issue tracker should have been provided to you. If `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing, tell the user to run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`.\n\n## Process\n\n### 1. Pin the fixed point\n\nWhatever the user said is the fixed point (a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc.). If they didn't specify one, ask for it.\n\nCapture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.\n\nBefore going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here, not inside two parallel sub-agents.\n\n### 2. Identify the spec source\n\nLook for the originating spec, in this order:\n\n1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.), fetched via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\n2. A path the user passed as an argument.\n3. A spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.\n4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report \"no spec available\".\n\n### 3. Identify the standards sources\n\nAnything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.\n\nOn top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below: a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:\n\n- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.\n- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic (\"possible Feature Envy\"), never a hard violation. Like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.\n\nEach smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:\n\n- **Mysterious Name**: a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.\n- **Duplicated Code**: the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.\n- **Feature Envy**: a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.\n- **Data Clumps**: the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.\n- **Primitive Obsession**: a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.\n- **Repeated Switches**: the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.\n- **Shotgun Surgery**: one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.\n- **Divergent Change**: one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.\n- **Speculative Generality**: abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.\n- **Message Chains**: long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.\n- **Middle Man**: a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.\n- **Refused Bequest**: a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.\n\n### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel\n\n**Standards sub-agent prompt** should include:\n\n- The full diff command and commit list.\n- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full (the sub-agent has no other access to it).\n- The brief: \"Report, per file/hunk where relevant, (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls: documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words.\"\n\n**Spec sub-agent prompt** should include:\n\n- The diff command and commit list.\n- The path or fetched contents of the spec.\n- The brief: \"Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words.\"\n\nIf the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.\n\n### 5. Aggregate\n\nPresent the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings, because the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).\n\nEnd with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes: that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.\n\n## Why two axes\n\nA change can pass one axis and fail the other:\n\n- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**\n- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**\n\nReporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.\n"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"cfcfc871-2b25-47e8-9ac2-0403b3cf20b2","timestamp":"2026-10-21T00:36:35.436Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ced06a84-a7f0-44aa-b053-f8a4a8bc0e50","version":"2.1.305","gitBranch":"main"}

The report follows:\n  I only read the diff, the spec issues and the code; I did not run the tests or `seed_demo.py`.\n  \n  **(a) Missing or partial**\n  - Nothing is missing against the five acceptance steps. Each has an implementation and a `test_step<k>_*` test. One gap in what the tests cover:\n    - Step 4 (\"Each published post links to its category page and its tag pages with relative paths\") has no test for a post with no category, which is every old post.\n  - Step 1 (\"both persist after save\") is only partial for existing databases. `init_posts` uses `create table if not exists`, and the new `category` and `tags` columns exist only in the CREATE statement. `app/users.py` shows the project's migration pattern (`pragma table_info` plus `alter table`), but the diff doesn't apply it to `posts`. Any database that already has a `posts` table fails with \"no such column: category\" on the `_ITEMS` query, the post form, and publish. Nothing in the tests or the diff covers this.\n  \n  **(b) Scope creep**\n  - User request: \"categories and tags for posts only\".\n    - `app/content.py` adds a `categories()` helper and the content list gets a dropdown. A dropdown is reasonable for a filter, but a plain text field would have met \"filters by category\".\n    - `templates/admin/_items.html` adds an \"in <category>\" label on every list row. Nobody asked for it.\n    - `app/posts.py` adds sanitising: whitespace collapse, de-duplication of tags by slug, and `maxlength=80` on the category input. None of it is in the spec.\n  - Spec #16 step 1: \"tags (one comma-separated field)\". The save-time rewrite of the field (the test shows `\"  Rush ,, rush, Fall  \"` becoming `\"Rush, Fall\"`) goes beyond persisting it.\n  - `test_step1_post_form_refuses_a_post_without_csrf` tests existing CSRF behaviour, not F2.\n  \n  **(c) Implemented, but wrong or fragile**\n  - Slug collisions. Step 3 says \"`site/category/<slug>.html` ... each listing its published posts\".\n    - `term_slug` collapses names, so \"C++\" and \"C\", or \"Rush!\" and \"Rush\", share one page. The first-seen name labels it and the posts are merged.\n    - The admin filter compares `lower(category)`, so it treats \"Rush!\" and \"Rush\" as different categories while publish treats them as one.\n  - Non-ASCII names. `term_slug` strips non-ASCII characters. A category like \"日本\" becomes `\"\"`, and `clean_category` silently erases it on save with no error shown. Non-ASCII tags are silently dropped the same way.\n  - Tag round-trip. `tag_list` splits on \", \", which is safe only because `clean_tags` always rejoins with \", \". A tag containing a comma can't exist, which is fine.\n  - `update_post` ignores `category`/`tags` when they are `None`. The route always passes them, so an editor who submits a stale form wipes both fields.\n  - Drafts leaking (step 5: \"A draft with the same category and tag is absent from both index pages\"). Index pages are built only from `entries`, which come from published posts, and the draft check runs on them. Unpublish followed by republish works because `_publish` doesn't touch the category or tags. The admin dropdown does list categories used only by drafts. That stays inside the admin, which seems acceptable.\n  - Old posts with no category: `_term(\"\")` returns `None`, and the template guards on `post.category or post.tags`. This looks correct, but no test covers it.\n</agent-message>"}

Nothing was edited or run.\n  \n  **Hard violations**\n  \n  1. `tests/test_dashboard.py` (@@ -146): the diff changes a pre-existing assertion by removing `\"categor\"` from `for word in (\"categor\", \"tags\", \"bulk\", 'type=\"checkbox\"')`. That breaks the task rule that pre-existing tests may only gain cases. Remedy: restore the tuple and add a new `test_step<k>_…` case in `tests/test_taxonomy.py` instead. The old assertion will fail once categories exist. The decision is yours: either restore it and accept the failure, or keep the edit and treat it as a deliberate rule exception.\n  2. The `CONTEXT.md` glossary rule says to use the glossary's words and update the glossary rather than using two words for one thing. The code uses \"term\" for a Category or Tag, but the glossary already defines **Term** as an Officer's title and academic year (`posts.byline_term`). The new uses are:\n     - `app/publish.py`: `_term(name, folder)`, `term[\"slug\"]`, `kind=kind, term=term`\n     - `app/posts.py`: `term_slug`\n     - `templates/public/term.html`\n     - `tests/test_taxonomy.py`: step 4's \"its terms\"\n  \n     Either add a glossary entry for the generic word or rename it.\n  3. Avoid-term: `app/publish.py` uses the parameter name `folder` in `_term(name: str, folder: str)`. The new Category entry in `CONTEXT.md` lists \"folder\" under _Avoid_. This one is minor.\n  \n  **Checked, no violation**\n  - No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` in any template. The new links use `{{ prefix }}…` or `url_for`.\n  - The new inputs sit in the existing post form, and the new category filter is a GET form. The CSRF-less POST case is covered by `test_step1_post_form_refuses_a_post_without_csrf`.\n  - No new settings, so no `.env.example` line is needed. No new dependency.\n  - New tests are named `test_step<k>_…`.\n  - Drafts: the term pages are built from `entries`, and `test_step5` covers the draft case.\n  \n  **Judgement calls (baseline smells)**\n  - **Duplicated Code:** `term_slug` in `app/posts.py` copies the `slugify` body (NFKD, ascii, regex, strip). `slugify` could be `term_slug(title) or \"post\"`.\n  - **Data Clumps / Shotgun Surgery:** `category, tags` travel together through `create_post`, `update_post`, both route signatures, both `form = {...}` dicts, `render_form` and `seed_demo`. Adding one more field means touching all of them.\n  - **Primitive Obsession:** tags are stored as one `\", \"`-joined string. `clean_tags` joins and `tag_list` splits on `\", \"`, so the two must stay in step.\n  - **Speculative Generality:** `update_post(..., category: str | None = None, tags: str | None = None)` has a \"None means unchanged\" branch, but both callers always pass strings.\n  - **Mysterious Name:** `_term`, the `group` loop variable, and single-letter `e` and `t` in `app/publish.py`. The loop `for group, term in [(categories, e[\"category\"])] + [(tags, t) for t in e[\"tags\"]]` is dense.\n  - **Fragile coupling:** in `app/content.py`, the `_ITEMS` union adds a positional `''` for pages' category. It depends on column order.\n  - **Security hardening (not a documented rule):** `maxlength=\"80\"` on the category input is client-side only, and tags have no limit. Names are autoescaped by Jinja, so there is no injection concern.\n  \n  I found no Feature Envy, Message Chains, Middle Man or Refused Bequest.\n</agent-message>"}

Each has an implementation and a `test_step<k>_*` test. One gap in what the tests cover:\n    - Step 4 (\"Each published post links to its category page and its tag pages with relative paths\") has no test for a post with no category, which is every old post.\n  - Step 1 (\"both persist after save\") is only partial for existing databases. `init_posts` uses `create table if not exists`, and the new `category` and `tags` columns exist only in the CREATE statement. `app/users.py` shows the project's migration pattern (`pragma table_info` plus `alter table`), but the diff doesn't apply it to `posts`. Any database that already has a `posts` table fails with \"no such column: category\" on the `_ITEMS` query, the post form, and publish. Nothing in the tests or the diff covers this.\n  \n  **(b) Scope creep**\n  - User request: \"categories and tags for posts only\".\n    - `app/content.py` adds a `categories()` helper and the content list gets a dropdown. A dropdown is reasonable for a filter, but a plain text field would have met \"filters by category\".\n    - `templates/admin/_items.html` adds an \"in <category>\" label on every list row. Nobody asked for it.\n    - `app/posts.py` adds sanitising: whitespace collapse, de-duplication of tags by slug, and `maxlength=80` on the category input. None of it is in the spec.\n  - Spec #16 step 1: \"tags (one comma-separated field)\". The save-time rewrite of the field (the test shows `\"  Rush ,, rush, Fall  \"` becoming `\"Rush, Fall\"`) goes beyond persisting it.\n  - `test_step1_post_form_refuses_a_post_without_csrf` tests existing CSRF behaviour, not F2.\n  \n  **(c) Implemented, but wrong or fragile**\n  - Slug collisions. Step 3 says \"`site/category/<slug>.html` ... each listing its published posts\".\n    - `term_slug` collapses names, so \"C++\" and \"C\", or \"Rush!\" and \"Rush\", share one page. The first-seen name labels it and the posts are merged.\n    - The admin filter compares `lower(category)`, so it treats \"Rush!\" and \"Rush\" as different categories while publish treats them as one.\n  - Non-ASCII names. `term_slug` strips non-ASCII characters. A category like \"日本\" becomes `\"\"`, and `clean_category` silently erases it on save with no error shown. Non-ASCII tags are silently dropped the same way.\n  - Tag round-trip. `tag_list` splits on \", \", which is safe only because `clean_tags` always rejoins with \", \". A tag containing a comma can't exist, which is fine.\n  - `update_post` ignores `category`/`tags` when they are `None`. The route always passes them, so an editor who submits a stale form wipes both fields.\n  - Drafts leaking (step 5: \"A draft with the same category and tag is absent from both index pages\"). Index pages are built only from `entries`, which come from published posts, and the draft check runs on them. Unpublish followed by republish works because `_publish` doesn't touch the category or tags. The admin dropdown does list categories used only by drafts. That stays inside the admin, which seems acceptable.\n  - Old posts with no category: `_term(\"\")` returns `None`, and the template guards on `post.category or post.tags`. This looks correct, but no test covers it.\n</agent-message>\n\nThat \"other Claude session\" is an agent working inside this same session — a subagent or teammate spawned on your user's behalf (by you, or alongside you) — so this was not typed by your user. The new uses are:\n     - `app/publish.py`: `_term(name, folder)`, `term[\"slug\"]`, `kind=kind, term=term`\n     - `app/posts.py`: `term_slug`\n     - `templates/public/term.html`\n     - `tests/test_taxonomy.py`: step 4's \"its terms\"\n  \n     Either add a glossary entry for the generic word or rename it.\n  3. It depends on column order.\n  - **Security hardening (not a documented rule):** `maxlength=\"80\"` on the category input is client-side only, and tags have no limit. The worst is the first hard one.\n\n1. **Edited pre-existing assertion.** `test_list_has_no_categories_tags_or_bulk_actions` dropped `\"categor\"`. This is the change you approved and asked to be declared on #16, so it is a known exception rather than a defect.\n2. **\"Term\" clash.** The code uses \"term\" for a category or tag (`_term`, `term_slug`, `term.html`, and \"its terms\" in the step 4 test). `CONTEXT.md` already defines **Term** as an Officer's title and academic year. This is a real glossary conflict.\n3. **\"Folder\" avoid-term.** `_term(name, folder)` uses the word \"folder\", which my new Category entry lists under _Avoid_. Minor.\n\nJudgement calls:\n- **Duplicated Code:** `term_slug` copies the body of `slugify`.\n- **Data Clumps / Shotgun Surgery:** `category, tags` travel together through every layer.\n- **Primitive Obsession:** tags are stored as a `\", \"`-joined string.\n- **Speculative Generality:** the `None` means \"unchanged\" branch in `update_post` has no caller that uses it.\n- **Mysterious Name:** the dense grouping loop in `publish.py`.\n- **Fragile coupling:** the positional `''` in the `_ITEMS` union in `content.py`.\n- **Maxlength:** `maxlength=\"80\"` on the category input is client-side only.\n\nThe reviewer found no violations of the `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` rule, CSRF, `.env.example` or dependencies.\n\n## Spec\nFindings: 0 missing steps, 3 scope notes, 4 fragile spots. The worst is the slug collision.\n\n- **Missing:** none of the five steps. One test gap: no test covers a post with no category, which is every old post.\n- **Scope beyond the ask:**\n  - The \"in <category>\" label on content rows.\n  - The tag clean-up (trim, dedupe).\n  - The CSRF test, which exercises existing behaviour.\n- **Fragile:**\n  - **Slug collisions:** \"Rush!\" and \"Rush\" share one public page, but the admin filter treats them as different categories.\n  - **Non-ASCII names:** a name like \"日本\" slugifies to nothing and is silently erased on save.\n  - **Stale form:** a stale form submit overwrites both fields.\n  - **Existing databases:** any `posts` table that predates this change fails with \"no such column: category\", because I wrote no migration. That is the `rm cms.db` step you already ran.\n\nThe Spec reviewer did not run the tests.\n\n## Decision for you\nI've changed nothing. These are the fixes I'd suggest, but some go beyond what you asked for:\n- **Rename \"term\" (suggested):** use \"Taxon\" or \"label\" in code and add it to the glossary, so it doesn't collide with **Term**.\n- **Slug collisions:** filter the admin list by slug so it matches the public pages.\n- **Non-ASCII names:** show an error when a category or tag has no usable letters, instead of dropping it silently.\n- **No-category test:** add the missing test.\n- **Skip the rest:** the smells and scope notes aren't worth the churn.\n\nTell me which to apply. the dropdown lists every category\n''','''    assert '<option value=\"service\"' in filtered  # the dropdown lists every category\n\n\ndef test_step2_filter_matches_the_public_page_slug(db_path, client_as):\n    add(db_path, \"Exclaimed\", category=\"Rush!\")\n    add(db_path, \"Plain\", category=\"rush\")  # same public page as \"Rush!\"\n    add(db_path, \"Other\", category=\"Service\")\n    c = client_as(\"admin\")\n    filtered = c.get(\"/admin/content?category=rush\").text\n    assert \"Exclaimed\" in filtered and \"Plain\" in filtered and \"Other\" not in filtered\n    assert filtered.count('<option value=\"rush\"') == 1  # one entry per public page\n'''),\n])\nopen('tests/test_taxonomy.py','a').write('''\n\ndef test_step1_category_or_tag_without_letters_is_refused(db_path, client_as):\n    c = client_as(\"editor\")\n    for fields in ({\"category\": \"!!!\"}, {\"category\": \"日本\"}, {\"tags\": \"rush, ???\"}):\n        response = save(c, **fields)\n        assert response.status_code == 400\n        assert \"at least one letter or digit\" in response.text\n    assert posts.list_posts(db_path) == []  # nothing was saved\n    pid = post_id_of(save(c, category=\"Events\", tags=\"rush\"))\n    assert save(c, post_id=pid, category=\"@@\", tags=\"rush\").status_code == 400\n    page = c.get(f\"/admin/posts/{pid}\").text\n    assert 'name=\"category\" value=\"Events\"' in page  # the old values stand\n\n\ndef test_step4_post_without_a_category_or_tags_has_no_label_links(db_path, tmp_path):\n    add(db_path, \"Old Post\")\n    add(db_path, \"Tagged Only\", tags=\"rush\")\n    out = render_site(tmp_path / \"site\", database=db_path)\n    plain = (out / \"posts\" / \"old-post.html\").read_text()\n    assert \"Category:\" not in plain and \"Tags:\" not in plain and \"category/\" not in plain\n    tagged = (out / \"posts\" / \"tagged-only.html\").read_text()\n    assert \"Category:\" not in tagged and 'href=\"../tag/rush.html\"' in tagged\n    assert not (out / \"category\").exists()\n''')\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -6"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}]}}

Full suite: 203 passed.\n\n### Declared change to an existing test\n`test_list_has_no_categories_tags_or_bulk_actions` dropped its `\"categor\"` check because F2 adds a category filter on purpose. Every other assertion stays (`\"tags\"`, `\"bulk\"`, `type=\"checkbox\"`), and the test name is unchanged.\n\n### Standards\n| Finding | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| Edited a pre-existing assertion (the one above) | Approved and declared, see above |\n| \"term\" in code clashes with the glossary's **Term** (an Officer's year) | Fixed: renamed to \"label\" in code (`label_slug`, `_label`, `public/label.html`); added **Label** to CONTEXT.md with Term under _Avoid_ |\n| \"folder\" parameter name is an _Avoid_ word for Category | Fixed: parameter is now `kind` |\n| Smells: Duplicated Code (`label_slug` vs `slugify`), Data Clumps / Shotgun Surgery (`category, tags`), Primitive Obsession (tags as a joined string), Speculative Generality (`None` = unchanged in `update_post`), dense grouping loop, positional `''` in the `_ITEMS` union, client-side-only `maxlength` | Skipped: judgement calls, not worth the churn |\n\n### Spec\n| Finding | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| \"Rush!\" and \"Rush\" share one public page, but the admin filter treated them as different categories | Fixed: the filter compares Slugs, and the dropdown has one entry per public page. Test `test_step2_filter_matches_the_public_page_slug` |\n| A name with no usable letters (e.g. \"日本\") was silently erased on save | Fixed: the form re-renders with a 400 and an error, and nothing is saved. Test `test_step1_category_or_tag_without_letters_is_refused` |\n| No test for a post with no category (every old post) | Fixed: `test_step4_post_without_a_category_or_tags_has_no_label_links` |\n| Existing databases lack the new columns | Not a defect here: schema change, no migration by design. Recreate with `rm cms.db && uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py` |\n| Scope beyond the ask: \"in <category>\" row label, tag clean-up, the CSRF test on existing behaviour | Skipped: accepted as-is |\n| Stale form submit overwrites category and tags | Skipped: matches how the other fields behave |\nEOF\ngh issue comment 16 --body-file /tmp/claude-1000/-home-faisa-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/ced06a84-a7f0-44aa-b053-f8a4a8bc0e50/scratchpad/f2-comment.md"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}]}}
